# What this repo checks, and why

Index of every check in `bench/`. One row per script: what it defends, what it
needs to run, and whether it has earned trust.

There is no test suite. Each script is standalone, prints its own `ok` /
`FAIL` lines, and returns a non-zero exit code on failure.
`bench/run_checks.py` runs them all with the card masked and compares what is
red with `bench/checks_baseline.json` ("Running them").

**Per-check narrative and the run logs live in
[`check_postmortems.md`](check_postmortems.md)**, not here. This file is
the index and the standard. It carries no run history and no counts in prose:
the table below is the authority for what exists, and a count typed into a
sentence here has been wrong every time it was tried.

**Two entries are not a `check_*.py`**: `bench/preflight_graph.py` and
`bench/build_wiki_index.py`, listed last. The first grades and prices rather
than gating, and a person runs it before a render rather than the suite running
it. The second reports the documents no link reaches.
Listing both is deliberate -- each defends real ground -- and it is why
"every check" and "every row" are not the same set.

## The standard

A check reads the thing under test, not its own copy of the answer (see
"Drive the expression, never restate it" below), and compares it with an
independent answer where one exists: a vendor file, a committed baseline, a
second implementation. One that reports red while the state is correct is
worse than no check. Where the expected value is itself the measurement, as
with `check_correctness.py`'s error against the stock forward, the check
records it rather than inventing a threshold.

**Showing a check red is not required** (the owner retired that requirement and
the index's `shown red` column on 2026-09-11; git history has what each cell
recorded). When a session does break something to show a check red, use
`bench/break_checks.py`, because the break is evidence only under three
conditions, each learnt from a run that reported breaks it had never seen
(2026-10-10 and 2026-10-16):

- **beside a green run of the same unbroken copy by the same command.** A copy
  that cannot import ComfyUI is red whatever was broken; `break_checks.py`
  makes the copy where a check can import what it imports in the tree, runs the
  control first, and calls a red with an import error the wrong reason. A
  hand-made copy has to sit under a folder named `custom_nodes`, or it cannot
  import the package.
- **when the failing line names the behaviour that was broken.** A break caught
  only by one broad case (eight removed refusals all failing the same "a key
  left uncarried is refused") shows that something changed, not that each guard
  has its own case.
- **one break per copy.** Each break reruns the whole check, and two breaks in
  one copy overwrite each other's job file, so a parallel run needs a copy each.

### A metric that ranks two arms is a claim about the metric

**A ranking is a property of the metric until you have named what the metric is
blind to**, and the naming has to happen before the ranking is published. Adopted
2026-08-31, after two wrong conclusions were published in consecutive commits on
one LoRA delta merged into an `int8_convrot` weight (`bench/measure_merge_realisation.py`;
records `bench/results/2026-08-31_merge_realisation_{pdd,turbo}.json`):

| metric | what it prefers | what it cannot see |
|---|---|---|
| stored-weight distance `‖Q(W+d) − (W+d)‖ / ‖W+d‖` | round-to-nearest | that RTN wins **because it barely applies the update** — the target is close to the unmerged weight, so doing nothing scores well |
| realisation `<Q(W+d) − Q(W), d> / <d, d>` | stochastic | that a sub-step delta is realised as sparse **full-step jumps** — the direction is right, the per-weight representation is not |
| noise against the update `‖Q(W+d) − (W+d)‖ / ‖d‖` | neither | (it is the one that shows both failures, and nobody was recording it) |

The third showed the shipped stochastic path applies the turbo LoRA and injects
**twelve times its magnitude in noise**. The cheap discipline: when a measurement
orders two arms, write down in the same sentence what would have to be true for
the loser to actually be better. If that sentence is hard to write, the ranking
is not ready.

**A subset preserves the centre and loses the tail.** Measured the same day: a
100-module half of a 200-module population reproduced the median to three
decimals (1.979 against 1.981) and got the minimum wrong by 3.2x (0.877 against
0.274). The loss had a direction: the kinds easiest to sample (`qkv_proj`, `fc2`)
were the least affected. Ask what makes a module easy to sample before trusting
a subset; the owner declined replacing the full sweep with stride sampling for
this reason, and the sweep is why the tail figures are real.

**What made it recoverable was a second implementation, not a second reading**:
a peer session wrote the statistic fresh rather than importing it, and the
agreement between implementations (worst-module 0.0199 against 0.01988 on
different subsets) is what made either number trustworthy.

### Drive the expression, never restate it

**A check that RESTATES the expression it grades cannot fail when the
expression is wrong.** It is grading its own copy, and the copy agrees with
itself no matter what the code does.

| the restated thing | what it hid |
|---|---|
| `check_pdd_sigmas::emitted()` recomputed `1 - block_bounds(...)` | dropping the `1.0 -` in `pdd_lora.py` left **every case in that file green** |
| the same file's refusal cases recomputed `num_steps % steps` | they asserted their own arithmetic, so no behaviour of the node could have reddened them |

The fix: **lift the expression into a module-level function and have the check
call it.** `emit_sigmas` and `resolve_emit_steps` exist for no other reason.
After the lift, dropping the `1.0 -` reddens three cases and a non-dividing count
reddens one.

The tell: if you can delete the implementation and the check still passes
because it computed the answer itself, it is not a check. This is the same rule
as "prefer a control": a control is a second, independent answer; a restatement
is the same answer twice.

### A node tested on the queue has to be made to run, in the state under test

**A test that runs a node through the queue must make the executor actually run
it in the state under test.** The executor serves a node from its cache when its
class and inputs are unchanged, whatever its id, and runs each node once per
prompt, usually before any model of that prompt is loaded. Change an input to
force the run, and have the test report the state it ran in.

It escaped twice in one afternoon (2026-10-07), on `sam31_corrections.py`:

| the test | why it could not fail |
|---|---|
| the node's first acceptance on a queue: three rounds, an H3 sample between them | the node ran once per prompt before anything was loaded, then came from the cache; its fault needed it to run while its own earlier output was loaded |
| the step added for that fault: a second node of the same class on the same loader output | the same inputs, so the executor handed it the first node's output and never ran it; the step passed on the faulty file |

The fix is in `bench/sam31_corrections_queue_test.py::rerun_graphs`: the second
node differs in one input, and the probe reports what the shared module showed
before the call, so a pass names the state it passed in.

## Running them

Most need neither CUDA nor a model and finish in about a second.

```bash
# from the repo root, with the ComfyUI venv python
python bench/check_keyframe_canvas.py
```

**Before a commit that changes a node schema, a default or a loader, run the
sweep:**

```bash
<comfy venv python> bench/run_checks.py            # every check_*.py, masked
<comfy venv python> bench/run_checks.py --only sol --logs <dir>
<comfy venv python> bench/run_checks.py --changed  # what the changed files can turn red
```

**A sweep and a commit are two steps** (owner, 2026-10-05). The sweep runs in
the background; nothing is called green until its result has been read; and
the commit is a separate foreground command, never chained to the sweep with
`&&`. A chained commit lands on whatever the sweep's last line happened to
exit with, and a foreground sweep holds the session for its whole length.

**`--changed` is the quick form, and it widens itself.** It runs the checks
the working tree's changed files can turn red: a changed check, every check
whose source names a changed file, and the few that read the whole tree
(`bench/run_checks.py::ALWAYS`). When a node file is among the changes (a root
module, anything under `workflows/`, a vendored config, the harness itself;
`bench/run_checks.py::is_node_file`) it runs every check, because nothing short
of reading the diff can say a root module's change is not a schema change. It
prints `TARGETED` or `FULL` and why each check was chosen. `--since <commit>`
adds the files changed since a commit.

It prints one row per check (exit code, seconds, and the line that explains
a result that is not green) and exits 1 only when a check is red that
`bench/checks_baseline.json` does not name. That file lists the checks that
are red for a stated reason, one line each; an entry whose check is no longer
red is printed as STALE and should be deleted. **Exit 2 from a check is
"nothing graded"** (a card, a dataset, a directory or a server that is not
there) and is never a failure of the sweep. Every check is started with
`CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=` and with `H3_CHECK_SWEEP=1`, which the one check that
would queue a render reads and refuses on. It does not run `smoke_h3.py`, and
it does not run the card cases: the checks that need the card report 2, and
running those unmasked on a free card is a separate step.

**No check needs `PYTHONPATH`.** Those that import comfy put the ComfyUI root
on `sys.path` themselves (`bench/_lib::bootstrap`), and the rest never import it.

**`bench/_lib` is the shared helper.** `bootstrap()` puts the ComfyUI root on
`sys.path` and core on its CPU path when no card is visible; `needs(what,
present)` exits 2 with "nothing graded: needs ..." when a card, a dataset or an
environment variable is absent; and `case(name, fn)` with `finish()` print one
`ok` / `FAIL` line per case and return the exit code. A new check uses them. An
old check keeps the harness it has; nobody is asked to port one. Its module
docstring is the reference.

**Run `smoke_h3.py` after any change to the generator.** It is the only check
that submits, and the class of bug it catches -- a graph every validator passes
and the server refuses -- is invisible to everything else here. Free the GPU
(`POST /free` with `unload_models`) before the CUDA checks or they OOM and the
failure looks like a regression.

**While a render is on the card, mask it.** Every check and the generator
run with `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=` (empty) while another process is rendering.
This box always has a card, so "run only the CPU cases" is not a choice the
scripts can make for you: the Sol checks gate on `torch.cuda.is_available()`
and walk into their kernel cases the moment a card is visible, and the
generator's import of `comfy.model_management` opens a CUDA context to size
VRAM before a single graph is built (both reached a busy card on 2026-09-04).

```bash
CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES= python bench/check_sol_node_equivalence.py   # sink cases only
CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES= <comfy venv python> workflows/build_workflows.py
```

A check that needs the card exits 2 when masked; that is the expected reading,
not a failure. The generator takes ComfyUI's CPU path when no device is visible,
and so does a check that calls `bootstrap()`; a check that imports core without
that switch crashes in a masked sweep.

## The index

| check | defends | needs | claims block |
|---|---|---|---|
| `check_correctness.py` | the patched H3 forward against the stock one; mean relative error 0.0732 on both the eager norm path and the fused RMSNorm+RoPE path | CUDA; exits 2 without it (it returned 0 with "skipping" until 2026-10-05) | no |
| `check_clone_v_wiring.py` | `clone_v` reaches the forward, and only on modes that earn it | CUDA; exits 2 without it (same note) | no |
| `check_override_routing.py` | which calls the attention override sends to sage and which it declines, including the fallback when the kernel raises | - | yes |
| `check_lowvram_handoff.py` | **more than its name says.** Three cases are KJNodes interop (the `[x]` list hand-off, the fallback with a list input, `minimax_head_chunks` honoured from `transformer_options`); **two are ours regardless of KJNodes** -- the plain tensor path, and that head chunking at 1/2/3/7 groups reassembles bit-identically | - | yes |
| `check_vendor_config.py` | that `vendor_config/` still is what the release ships and still parses. Two failure modes needing different evidence: a vendored file edited by hand, caught by hashing against `vendor_config/sha256.json`; and the release changing underneath, caught only by diffing the published repo, which is 200 GB and lives outside this tree. **When the release is not reachable it says so in as many words rather than passing quietly** -- a skipped comparison that looks like a passed one is the failure this check is shaped against. Also exercises the readers, because a file that hashes correctly and then yields no bound is still broken, and `vendor_config.py` raises rather than defaulting so this can see it. Point it at the release with `--release` or `H3_RELEASE_DIR`; neither is written into the repo | `vendor_config/`, plus the release when it is on disk | no |
| `check_schema_defaults.py` | every node's `io.Schema` defaults match its `execute()` signature defaults. ComfyUI does **not** inject a schema default for an input a prompt omits, so the two are independent and a split means the UI and the API path see different values | `PYTHONPATH` self-bootstrapped | yes |
| `check_ref_prompt_labels.py` | every ref graph's prompt names **exactly** the labels its graph wires. The tokenizer derives `<Picture i>` / `<Video k>` / `<Audio j>` from the wired sockets, not from the prompt, so the two drift silently -- and a video's soundtrack takes `<Audio 1>` ahead of a standalone clip, which is easy to number wrong by hand. **`wired_labels` counted only `ref_*` sockets until 2026-08-21**, so a keyframe graph's correct `<Picture 1>` read as a label no socket wires; keyframes are `<Picture N>` too, emitted from the plain `images=` list at `comfy/text_encoders/minimax.py:183` in wiring order. **Also owns discovery**: a case added 2026-08-16 asserts no directory under `workflows/` holds graphs the shared walk cannot see. `assert graphs` alone could not catch that -- a PARTIAL walk satisfies it too, demonstrated the same day when a stale `GRAPH_DIRS` made this file and `check_prompt_guide_conformance` both exit 0 while covering 20 ref graphs instead of 28 | - | yes |
| `check_prompt_guide_conformance.py` | every shipped prompt against **the guide that applies to its graph**, parsed at run time -- the six sections and their order, the `[...]` task-type vocabulary and its ` + ` combining rule, markers never crossing the visual/audio sets, and `<d>` only in `detailed_description`. Exists because `check_ref_prompt_labels.py` compares the generator to itself and so passed clean while all fourteen arms shipped a hardcoded `[reference generation]`. The `keyframe completion` case checks the GRAPH, not the vocabulary: the type is allowed only where the graph wires `MiniMaxH3AddGuide` (added to ComfyUI 2026-08-13, and merged with refs additively by `comfy/model_base.py`, so the mechanism now exists). Before that node it was rejected outright on the grounds that nothing could honour it -- reasoning that expired the day the node landed. Exits 2, not 0, when the guide is absent. Carries a waiver list, `bench/check_prompt_guide_conformance.py::_STRUCTURE_PROBES`: a probe that is unstructured on purpose has its section and prefix cases skipped **by name and printed on every run** -- markers, dialogue placement and label agreement are still enforced, proven by mutating them. **Two changes on 2026-08-16.** The blanket waiver on the image graph was replaced by a structural rule: `overall_soundscape` and `non_diegetic_music` are not required of a graph with no `VAEDecodeAudio`, read off the graph rather than off a name, so the other four sections are now graded on the image path where a whole-file waiver had been buying silence on them. And the "in order" case now actually checks order -- it compared against a list built by iterating the guide's sections, so it could only ever detect a missing one. **The release ships TWO guides and this file knew one, until 2026-08-21.** It looked only for `MiniMaxH3ReferenceToVideo` and graded everything against ref-en's six sections, so the base-format population -- t2v and the keyframe path, which moved onto this repo's own `MiniMaxH3Conditioning` that day -- was not failing, it was invisible. Both halves are now read off the graph: a node wiring any `ref_*` socket is full-reference mode and gets ref-en, everything else gets base-en's three core fields. The cases that read `summary` and `retention_analysis` are exempt on base prompts, where those sections correctly do not exist, and **the exemption count prints on every run**. base-en carries no markdown table, so its vocabulary is parsed two independent ways -- the fenced example and the bulleted definitions -- and they must agree, or case 0 fails rather than grading every base prompt against an empty section list **Since 2026-10-01, also that every node that carries a `prompt` string is in `h3_config.PROMPT_INPUTS`**, read both from the shipped graphs and from the pack's node source, with a control that the same scan fails on a registry missing the song node, and a control that a graph with a song node is still asked for the two audio sections (it decodes audio itself, so it has no `VAEDecodeAudio`). Escaped instance: the list in `preflight_graph.py` was missing core's conditioner until 2026-08-21 and the song node until 2026-10-01, and each time "nothing to grade" read as a pass. | the guides in `vendor_guides/` | yes |
| `check_distill_settings.py` | **every** shipped graph runs its distill on that distill's own contract: PDD at the base 12/3 on its fused grid, FlashGen on its header's sigmas, FastH3 V2 at `FASTH3_SHIFT` on the template or the contract; a graph with no distill sits at the base 12/3. Also: every node of ours with a `lora_name` input is a known loader, and every audio-only refine pass runs undistilled. **Since 2026-09-26 a graph loading any turbo LoRA fails**: the lightx2v family was retired (`docs/state/roadmap.md`, "Closed lanes"), and its vendor-row grading (`LEGAL`, `UNATTESTED`, `OWNER_RECIPE`, the vendor-README and LightX2V-config controls, the exit-2 skip) left with it; the file as of 0.155.0 has them | - | yes |
| `check_distill_grid.py` | that a distilled graph samples **on** the sigma grid it was distilled at. `check_distill_settings.py` above grades the shift and the step count; both can be right while the scheduler still evaluates somewhere else, and nothing errors. PDD graphs are graded against the boundaries their fused heads were built at, both streams. The flow-shift rule itself is anchored externally: the rule and literal NFE=4 sigmas in `coderef/Minimax-H3-Turbo/README.md`'s "Note on shift" against ComfyUI's own `calculate_sigmas`, and `simple_is_the_only_one` asserts every other scheduler MISSES, so it cannot pass by everything agreeing. **Since 2026-09-26 it grades no turbo graph**: those cases (the divisor regime, owner-recipe arms, the exemption list and the split-arm shift rule) left with the lightx2v lane. No split graph exists; `build_api(split_at=...)` still builds one, and a new split graph would need that shift rule back | ComfyUI importable and `coderef/`; no CUDA, model or server. A missing README exits 2; an unreachable ComfyUI FAILS | yes |
| `check_solattn_correctness.py` | Sol-Attn's **CUDA kernel** against the algorithm's own eager reference, cosine > 0.998, graded in the kernel's own measured `centroid_tail` mode. **Scope narrowed 2026-08-16**: the Triton arms went with the pack. They graded a kernel no graph had wired since 2026-08-14, and worse, the CUDA arm was coupled to them by control flow alone -- the script loaded Triton first and returned 2 on failure, *before* the CUDA arm was reached, so an absent pack silently disabled the only correctness check on the kernel that does run. The tail mode is now measured BEFORE the graded cases so they use the matching oracle. Exits 2 when there is no CUDA or no `sol_attn`, which is the expected state on a machine that has not built the fork | CUDA and a fork build of comfy_kitchen | yes |
| `check_bench_matches_shipped.py` | that `bench_e2e_h3.py`'s `shipped` arm builds the same text-encoder loader/filename, dense-kernel node (sage or, since 2026-09-15, core's `ModelAttentionBackend`: presence and values, `sage_matches` and `backend_matches`) and Sol settings the graphs actually wire, node for node. Exists because `check_generator_constants.py` pins the **generator** and nothing pinned the **bench** -- a gap that cost a day of numbers when the `fp16 (most accurate)` flip landed everywhere except here | - | yes |
| `check_attention_defaults.py` | that every **graph** carries the attention configuration `h3_config` declares: Sol values against whichever config `h3_config.sol_for_graph` resolves for that graph (**two since 2026-08-29** -- `SOL_PDD_CUDA` for an arm that loads a PDD LoRA, `SOL_RECOMMENDED_CUDA` otherwise, and the check calls that resolver rather than re-deriving it), sage values against `SAGE_NODE`, the Sol-on-by-default rule as **reachability from an output node**, not presence, and **since 2026-09-15 the dense floor under Sol**: core's `ModelAttentionBackend` values against `DENSE_BACKEND_NODE`, and every graph that runs on sage or stock attention instead named in `FLOOR_STEMS` with a mechanism, asserted both ways like the exemptions. Complements rather than replaces `check_bench_matches_shipped.py`, whose subject is the bench harness -- neither implies the other, and on 2026-08-13 the config and graphs moved together while the bench stayed behind. Retires the **one graph, by accident** entry below: that was `check_bench_matches_shipped.py` noticing Sol only because its single `SOL_GRAPH` lost a node. Leaving the node out of the graph is how a shipped graph turns Sol off, and is not a defect; a node present that nothing consumes is, because ComfyUI walks backwards from the outputs so it never executes -- measured at a real cost in `bench/results/2026-08-18_attention_defaults.json`. **The exemption list cannot rot**: the single-frame class is derived from `GRAPH_DIRS` rather than named -- **empty since that path was parked on 2026-08-27**, and left derived so that re-adding a directory re-arms it -- and every exemption is asserted NECESSARY, so an exempt graph that gains live Sol goes red instead of being quietly covered. `workflows/bench/*_stamped_api.json` are outside `graph_paths()` and so stated as out of scope rather than exempted, because an exemption implies coverage | - | yes |
| `count_packed_rows.py` | **not a check — a counter, listed here because three checks and two docs quote numbers it is the authority for.** Builds the real `PackedLayout` and reads segment rows off it, so video, target audio and every reference are exact rather than derived. Confirmed `docs/attention/sol_attn.md`'s 16.6% sink share by a second path (256 blocks of 1,539) and reproduced the 2026-08-17 capture's sequence to zero residual. **Refuses to guess `text`**, which needs the encoder: `--text` takes a measured value and the report says so when it is absent, because a tool that invented one would recreate the defect it was built to find. Reference sizes go through `reference_fit._fit` rather than local arithmetic — the first draft reimplemented the snap-then-truncate rule, truncated only, and under-priced a 777-row reference as 720 | `PYTHONPATH` to ComfyUI. No CUDA, no model, no server | n/a — reports, does not assert |
| `check_node_ids.py` | **the rule CLAUDE.md opens with**, and the only check here whose baseline is deliberately hand-maintained. Every `node_id` plus the *ordered* input and output names, against `bench/node_id_manifest.json`. Covers both halves of the positional contract: renames, and the "append only" rule for inputs and outputs. **Its baseline is a committed file precisely because it must not be regenerated** -- graphs here are generated from the schema, so a rename regenerates every graph consistently and the generator's `/object_info` revalidation and the smoke both stay green while the owner's graphs *outside* the repo break silently. A control whose input is derived from the thing it checks cannot fail, so the guard had to sit outside that derivation. Two partial catches existed before it and neither sufficed: `bench_e2e_h3.py` hardcodes **2 of 8** ids (submit-time, GPU, server), and a rename shows in `git diff` across every generated graph (human-visible, machine-checked by nothing). Default on red is **revert**; `--write` is only for the two changes CLAUDE.md permits **An appended output is flagged like an appended input since 2026-10-05.** It was not: the inputs had an APPENDED branch and the outputs had none, so `MiniMaxH3SubjectTrack` gained its fourth output with the manifest one short and this check stayed green, printing that every output position was unchanged (seen by a peer session the same day) | `PYTHONPATH`, ComfyUI importable. No server, no CUDA | yes |
| `check_pdd_head_selection.py` | **the PDD node's runtime guards, on real artifacts.** Four subjects. First, the fused-head selector: driven with a real `adaln_t_table` off a shipped checkpoint at EVERY step count the 32-point grid divides by, video and audio separately because they run different shifts. **Rewritten 2026-08-27 to drive it through `observe` with a real sigma schedule**, so the step count is DERIVED the way a render derives it rather than handed in by the test -- plus cases that the derived knots equal what the retired `nfe` widget computed (`torch.equal` at every divisor), that an uneven schedule is taken and reported rather than refused, that `denoise < 1.0` starts partway down the trajectory, and that the surviving `nfe` override actually overrides. Note what the divisor sweep does NOT say: it grades the selector, not whether a 16-interval block is a sensible arm. That range is the point -- one converted file serves 8, 4, 2 and 16 evaluations by fusing at load, and a selector only ever seen at one block size is not evidence about the others. Earned by an escaped defect: on 2026-08-26 four arms rendered with every gate green and the node's own warning silent while steps 1 and 7 decoded the wrong block, because the selector recovered `t` from the curve table and a `t` sitting exactly on a boundary came back just below it. That selector is gone -- the node matches the boundary embeddings directly, so there is no `t` to quantise and no snap tolerance to justify. Second, both hand-set tolerances are graded against the noise each straddles, a bf16 cast below and the other partition above: `TABLE_TOLERANCE` shipped for an hour at 1e-3, BELOW the 1.6e-3 a cast costs, which would have rejected every correct adaln bake into the slower path silently, and `PARTITION_TOLERANCE` replaced a sha256 that fired on the CORRECT checkpoint for the same reason. Third, added 2026-08-27, the `final_layer.forward` object patch is arity-transparent: Comfy-Org/ComfyUI#15908 widens that method to seven parameters and an object patch REPLACES it, so a pinned four-parameter replacement drops three arguments the stock forward requires and dies on step 1. Asserts today's signature and the PR's, extras forwarded verbatim; needs no checkpoint, so it runs even when the rest SKIPs. **Added 2026-09-15: a re-executed PDD node does not chain onto the previous render's `final_layer` wrapper.** Earned by a false boundary warning and duplicate trace lines on an 8-step render after a 5-step one: the wrapper took its base from the live attribute, which is the earlier render's wrapper while that patcher stays applied. On core's real `ModelPatcher` with an earlier patcher applied, a wrapper on the live attribute runs the stale tracker (the control) and one on `get_model_object` does not; statically, every site that wraps a forward it read back (`pdd_lora.py`, `audio_carry_probe.py`, the `nodes.py` capture tap) reads it through `get_model_object`. Shown red against the pre-fix tree | both pruned partitions on disk, `safetensors`. No CUDA, no server, no model load; the arity cases need neither; the chaining case needs `comfy.model_patcher`, and sets ComfyUI's CPU flag when no card is visible so it runs masked | yes |
| `check_output_dir_resolution.py` | **that no script reaches the render output directory by counting `..`.** Earned by an instance that cost a full batch: `grade_pdd_partitions.py` resolved `parents[2] / "output"`, which from `bench/` is `custom_nodes/output`, so seven arms rendered at 362 frames and every one was thrown away undecoded -- the path was not touched until after the queue drained. The reason it is easy to write is that two conventions are in use and differ by one: `Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]` is `custom_nodes`, `HERE.parents[2]` with `HERE` the directory is the ComfyUI root. `h3_config.output_dir()` is now the one owner; it honours `H3_OUTPUT_DIR` (required on this box, which starts the server with `--output-directory` and exposes it through no API) and RAISES on a missing directory, which is the half that matters. **Deliberately narrow**: it flags only a path expression that counts parents AND names `output`, and does not police the `sys.path` bootstraps that reach the ComfyUI root by counting -- those run before `h3_config` is importable, so demanding they use it would demand a cycle | nothing; pure text scan | yes |

A re-upload of the alibaba-pai source keeps `proj_out.weight` and changes what is inside it, so both encodings have identical keys and key-branching is blind by construction; and that file's `our_bank_is_the_published_stack` grades our bank against the file it was copied from, which agrees with itself under either encoding. The observable is therefore the values: median `||row_i||/||row_0||`, ~1.0 verbatim against ~0.02 for deltas, because consecutive heads differ by only 2-3% of a head's norm. The synthetic heads are built at that same 3% spread so the separation graded is the separation that exists. Defines no arithmetic of its own -- it calls the converter's `bank_row_ratio` and `assert_bank_verbatim`, so deleting the guard reddens it | nothing. | `check_pdd_unmerged.py` | **that the un-merged backbone path computes the same thing as the merged one it replaces**, and therefore that the measured difference between them is attributable to quantisation rather than to a second model. `unmerged_blocks` moves a block's backbone LoRA from a weight patch to a forward patch; this asserts `unmerged(x) == x @ (W + (alpha/rank) * B @ A).T` on a stub Linear in float64. **The identity is the load-bearing part**: a merged patch on an int8_convrot module is dequantised, added and requantised with a recalculated scale, so IF the two paths agree in exact arithmetic the only thing left between them is that requantisation -- which is what `data/results/2026-08-31_pdd_quant_interaction_all_blocks.json` measures. This check grades the exact-arithmetic identity, where rounding does not appear at all, and the measurement it underwrites used deterministic rounding and says so -- so the real merged path is noisier than either. the held part fills an emptied and a misplaced frame exactly from its neighbours on a subject that walks, only the frames the caller chose when it chose, and leaves the middle of a long gap; from a class map the classes a part is made of are read back from its mask, each segment `<label>.<class>` is counted per frame, what lies inside a region without being the carried part is counted for the subject's own classes and another subject's, **and is gone once kept out**, and a class labelled on one subject in the strip beside its outline, inside another subject's track, is counted as the other's; and each preflight rule beside the case that must not raise it: a shot taken just above the match line against one well above, a shot called absent with somebody on screen against the same shot inside frames the caller says the subject is not in, a shot taken in frames the caller bars, what lies under a doubted part (the part, hair, another class, nothing) named from the class map, the look figure reading 0 at the source's level and 1 at a held render's, whose a pixel is (one claimant owns it, of two the one whose class map names it, named by both or by neither is contested and never guessed), a region carried across a cut by a latent step (the frames on the side the subject is not on, by the node's own frame runs, **and the same frames as `video_mask.cut_gate` when the tree has it**; nothing for a cut on a step's edge, a subject on both sides or a frame lost inside a shot), what a run changed on a frame whose difference from the source is known (a redrawn face, an untouched hairline and half of a neighbour's hand each at their own figure, a subject inside and outside the region told apart), a mouth and its timing (on drawn mouths open reads well above shut, unchanged by a tilted head or a stray label, larger on a smaller face; the same series two frames late is placed two frames late, and a mouth found elsewhere is not scored as the same one), a track empty only where the subject was taken, a part that is empty, spilled, shrunk or moved on single frames against **the same part held steady, which raises nothing**, and what `pieces=largest` took away from a part said a frame at a time with what it cannot see (the part-in-pieces flag is silent once the option is set), a voice sentence over unvoiced frames against a denial that is not read as one, and a sentence that names a reference picture (a retention line listing the mouth among the features kept) read as neither a voice nor its denial. **The readers and the gate**: a run's region read from the files its windows saved and from the node's own plan (a load rendered zoomed in read onto the canvas where the node lays it, 2026-10-13: the node's own cut of a box laid back is the mask it was cut from, on a grid and off one; a zoomed token lands on the canvas cell it covers and the cells are counted by three rules; the margin kept in canvas pixels with the node's figure beside it; a window of another box, a zoom for another canvas and a zoomed run's review each refused) (frames past the render's end dropped, a gap or another run's files refused, the kept frames of a run that continues a stored window read as that window's and not as a gap, a frame with no mask never laid, a zoomed run refused by name); a load shorter than its window whose held tail shows the original against the same tail left open; a face a few tokens tall against one that is not; a fill of a doubted part graded by the class map; a pose table as an input; a pass given as a list of loads; `verify` (two readers that agree against a shaved mask, a small mask not failed on plain overlap, a check that could not be made is not a pass, an empty frame a person's statement accounts for against one it does not cover, another subject's statement, and a load laid over frames a statement excused); the gate's verdict and an override held by what a flag is about and not its number; a named subject found on no frame of a load; a take under the tracker's own line against a typed one; a shot where some tracker's call is nobody's word, one flag carrying every tracker's question or the row's reason, against an answered row, one outside the span, and a file written before the who-is-who tool was cut to its reading half; an absence beside people another tracker holds, which is no question; whose pixels a part sits on; and the per-shot and per-load lists, where a flag counts only while it blocks and a clear cell still carries the take's state, likeness and size; several tracked subjects with no who-is-who file; a plan that writes a frame past its load against one that writes exactly its load; a planned load shorter than the span with nothing saying where it starts, refused; a part the class map vouches for and too small to be drawn, emptied by the grade; a frame a load reads with the subject tracked, its mask empty and no region, named, against a lent frame, an untracked one and one outside the load; | `check_reference_order.py` | the ordered-reference plan (`reference_order.py`) against **core's own bookkeeping**, as the acceptance suite for the typed surface that replaces socket wiring. Two verdicts, not one: AGREE that every socket configuration produces core's exact `ref_items` sequence, and DIFFER on the two behaviours the ordered model replaces on purpose -- suffix pairing becomes ownership, fixed iteration becomes list position. **A suite asserting AGREE on all seven contracts would reject a correct replacement for doing its job**, which is why `docs/research/2026-08-21_conditioning_nodes.md` splits the criteria. **Core is the authority, and `check_ref_prompt_labels.wired_labels` is NOT**: that function reduces soundtracks to a count and pairs `k < n_vaud`, so it cannot represent a sparse socket set and gets one wrong -- driven 2026-08-22, core emits `video, audio, video` for videos 0 and 1 with only `ref_video_audio_1` wired, pairing by suffix as `comfy_extras/nodes_minimax_h3.py:313-314` does, where `wired_labels` puts the track on the first clip. That disagreement had a case for the length of one slice and was **retired in the same slice that earned the retirement**: `wired_labels` is now a thin front end over `reference_order`, so the divergence is gone by construction and the case asserts they still share one function. That lifecycle is deliberately unlike the recorded core gaps elsewhere in this index -- those describe upstream code nobody here owns, where green-while-broken is the honest state; this was local code being fixed, where a case asserting it stayed broken would be green-while-broken by choice. It also covers the typed chain: tail-first traversal reversed into user order, interleaved records keeping their positions, a soundtrack owned rather than emitted as a standalone record, and **loud failure instead of a partial plan**. That contract took three rounds of adversarial probing to actually hold, every leak the same shape -- an assertion covering one side of a two-sided thing. Round 1 added cycles, non-append links and split frame/metadata ownership. Round 2 found a node wiring BOTH a chain and legacy sockets silently dropping the sockets. Round 3, from codex's executed probes, found five more: a declared `references` holding an empty list, or a link with no graph, or a malformed link, each returning `[]`; a mid-chain link from the wrong output slot being followed; and a video resolving with no `VHS_VIDEOINFO` at all, which makes the ownership claim -- that `loaded_fps` comes from owned metadata -- true for some records and not others with nothing saying which. **Key presence is now the discriminator**: a node declaring `references` IS an ordered node and never falls back to the legacy plan, because there is nothing to fall back TO. Frames and `video_info` must name their own output slots, since the right shape from the wrong output is the wrong value -- and the slot must be a real `int`, excluding `bool`, because `int(slot)` swallowed `0.9`, `"0"` and `True`. **The soundtrack deliberately has no fixed slot**, and that is round 4's finding: the rule required `[loader, 2]` where every sounded graph here routes through `TrimAudioDuration` and supplies `[trim, 0]`, so it rejected the entire shipped population. Caught by codex checking the rule against `workflows/` rather than against its own docstring. `loaded_fps` comes from the metadata, so frames and `video_info` stay strict; a soundtrack is EXPECTED to be processed, so its provenance is traced back through known pass-throughs instead and refused only when it reaches a **different known loader**. An unfamiliar audio node yields `soundtrack_origin="unresolved"`, carried on the record rather than discarded, because "we could not check" and "we checked and it is fine" must not read the same downstream. Compares ordered RECORDS rather than label sets, since a label set loses the sequence that is the actual contract -- and compares **both** of core's lists, because contract 1 is that `ref_items` and `ref_blocks` are not index-aligned: a sounded video is two presentation entries and one DiT block, so asserting only the first would pass a plan that labelled correctly and paid the wrong DiT cost | ComfyUI importable, stub VAEs; no CUDA, no model, no server | no |

Covers: Part One templates against the guide-parsed constants, camera vocabulary against the guide's 4.3 table, worked examples against `docs/prompting/prompting.md` §10 verbatim, **and every §10 example re-run through `preflight_graph.grade` at the duration its own heading names** -- because two documents agreeing says nothing about either being correct, and a bad example faithfully copied goes green on both. Quoted guide sentences against the guide. **The page is a second copy by construction and is published outside this repo**, which is why it is checked rather than trusted. Escaped instance: it drifted before its first check -- an example reworded in transcription, and seven camera motion types abbreviated. Compares whitespace-normalised, so line-wrapping changes are invisible by design; prose is not checkable in general and is not checked, apart from the pinned rule sentences below. **Dated snapshots are held to a different question**: never graded against current sources, since a frozen record falling behind is correct rather than wrong and grading it would be cry-wolf -- only that they stayed unmodified against `docs/prompting/portable/snapshots.json`, plus a snapshot-shaped file nothing records. Red-proved three ways: against the previously published page, an edited snapshot, and an unrecorded one **Since 2026-10-01, also twelve pinned rule sentences** (`RULE_PINS`): the load-bearing sentence of each rule that has drifted, mislabelled or been contested (the timestamp rule and its owner label, unison, never-vocalise, the silent-character phrase, the positional mouth cue, the `N/A` caution, the cinematography ruling, the listener rule, the agent rule, the specificity budget) must be present in the manual and in both portable copies. An in-memory mutation had inverted the timestamp, unison and never-vocalise rules in both copies and every one stayed green. It pins wording, not meaning, and says so; red-proved by ten inversions. | - | no |
| `check_skill_routes.py` | that every repo-relative path a `.claude/skills/*/SKILL.md` names in backticks exists. **A skill is an entry point**: a stale doc link makes a reader shrug, a dead skill route makes the agent that invoked it go nowhere, silently, while following the repo's own instructions. Escaped instance: `h3-experiment` step 4 routed prompt-writing to a gitignored directory that does not exist while `docs/prompting/prompting.md` was named by no skill at all. `check_doc_links.py` scans `.claude/` but its grammar is `path:line` citations and markdown links, and a bare backticked directory is neither; widening that grammar would false-red across the repo. Takes paths, never globs, so hand-built graphs are gradeable -- which is why it exists: the two prompt checks only ever saw `workflows/*_api.json`. **Reports, never refuses**, because a tool that blocks you in your own repo gets disabled. Names what it cannot count: a video reference is a floor, not a budget. It also ffprobes the media each socket resolves to and warns on a mono track (gap 7, which raises rather than degrading) -- reporting it, never fixing it, and reporting "unreadable" as its own state so a missing ffprobe cannot read as a pass **Since 2026-10-01 it reads every prompt carrier from `workflows/prompts.py::carriers`**, so a song graph is graded as the texts its Prompt Lists expand to (a placeholder with no list, or a list nothing uses, is a FAIL, as the node refuses both) and no longer answers "nothing to grade"; pricing is not modelled for the song node and the output says so. | reference images on disk; no CUDA, no model, no server | no |
| `smoke_h3.py` | the H3 chain composes and runs, after any node-pack update. **The only thing here that actually POSTs a prompt**, and on 2026-08-13 it was the only reason anyone discovered every API graph was unsubmittable -- `validate_api` was asserting the wrong shape, so no static check could have found it | live ComfyUI, GPU, model | no |

**2026-08-23 amendments to the long rows above.**
`check_reference_order.py` now admits only the four served VHS video-loader
classes, even when an unrelated node happens to expose values at slots 0 and
3. Soundtrack provenance follows both branches of `JoinAudioChannels`;
`foreign` is diagnostic rather than refused because ownership is the video's
typed record. `preflight_graph.py` now reads typed append chains as well as
legacy sockets, and base Part One is exact: mode, final shot, and snapped
duration are resolved against templates parsed from the release guide. Its red
harness now carries the plausible wrong-mode, wrong-duration, and wrong-shot
mutations that presence-only grading missed. These clauses supersede the older
soundtrack-refusal and Part-One-presence wording retained inside the historical
rows.

The shipped-reference migration supersedes the older explicit-trim wording in
the `preflight_graph.py` row. All generated reference workflows now use the
typed compiler, whose duration cap and mono normalization are derived at run
time; preflight still audits explicit trims on native socket graphs. The Qwen
bounds audit follows typed image append chains and refuses partial resolution.

Release video sizing is local policy, not native status. The runtime check
pins the real processor's raw 31-versus-padded-32 boundary and the separate
VAE/Qwen views; preflight marks `comfy` or `release` as active and calls the
latter a local typed policy. Native core remains the `comfy` row.

"claims block" means the file carries a `Claims, i.e. what breaks if a case is
deleted:` header enumerating what each case defends. The table, rather than a
count copied into prose, records which files carry one.


## What is deliberately not checked

`docs/state/open_experiments.md` is the other half of this document: the things
this repo has decided **not** to measure, each with its cost, the decision it
would change, and the actual blocker. Read it before proposing a new check --
several obvious ideas are already there with a reason attached.

A suite of twelve **render** scenes is designed but not run. They are quality
gates for output, not code checks: each carries a pre-registered binary claim
and a predicted per-arm direction, and a human watches and listens. Judging
whether a third shot happened or two voices stayed distinct is not a
`check_*.py`. They live in `internal/`, which is gitignored and not distributed:
they are the owner's working research notes, not shipped content.

**Two-stage split graphs do not exist** (the lightx2v ones were retired
2026-09-26), so nothing checks them. When one does, the checks worth writing
are: both stages read one schedule, stage 2 carries `DisableNoise`, the split
point is inside the step range, and the two stages' shift nodes agree.

### Citations that cannot resolve, for `check_doc_links.py`

The ledger that check reads, kept here rather than in the script for the same
reason `retraction-consumers` lives in [`evidence.md`](evidence.md): the
enumeration belongs beside the prose it governs, and nothing gets a second copy.

An entry asserts that someone looked and the target is *deliberately* gone. It
is not a way to silence a broken link.

## Retired checks

- **`check_void_conversion.py` and `check_void_conditioning.py`, deleted
  2026-10-05 with the VOID code, the day they were written.** The first held
  a checkpoint converter's key rename to core's CogVideoX model; the second
  held a conditioning node to upstream VOID's arithmetic. The owner had VOID
  tested as a clean-plate tool for the masked lane, judged every clip, and had
  the code removed (`docs/state/decisions.md`, 2026-10-05; the record is
  `bench/results/2026-10-05_void_plate_turn.md`). Their subjects are gone, so
  nothing replaces them.
- **`check_workflow_schema.py`, deleted 2026-09-14 with the UI workflows.** It
  validated the saved UI graphs, positional `widgets_values`, against a live
  `/object_info`. Shipped workflows have been API format only since that day.
  `workflows/build_workflows.py::validate_api` grades every graph by input name
  against the same live schema before anything is written, and the owner's
  editor-saved graphs outside the repo keep `check_node_ids.py`.
- **`check_uncontrolled_claims.py`, built and deleted 2026-09-01.** It asserted
  that no row of the table below claimed "nothing" guards a numbered guide
  section some `bench/check_*.py` already names. Across `docs/`, exactly one such
  line existed, and correcting that row was the fix; the check then scanned zero
  subjects on every run, and widening it needs judgement about prose, which
  means false reds. **A check that cannot fail is not a cheap safety net, it is
  a green light nobody earned.**

## Uncontrolled requirements

The standing audit. For each imperative a person could violate in code, the
question is *what assertion goes red if someone ignores this?* Only rows that
are open or partly open are listed; a requirement a check now holds is in the
index above, under that check. At a sweep, every row reading "nothing" is either
given a control or re-stated as a deliberate decision not to build one with the
reason recorded (adopted 2026-08-28; [`sustainability.md`](sustainability.md)
argues the case). The last sweep scheduled here was for 2026-09-28. The old
`CLAUDE.md` was swept; root `AGENTS.md`, its successor, and `docs/` have not been.

| requirement | enforced by |
|---|---|
| **`h3_trace.py`'s five route outcomes name the path that actually ran** -- `sage`, `sage_chunked`, `fallback_kernel`, `fallback_chunked`, `sol_delegate`, each written at its own exit in `attention.py` | **nothing.** Nobody has forced each of the five and confirmed where it lands. The sibling field `module` was found hardcoded `"dit"` on 2026-09-08 (every refiner call labelled a DiT one), which is an escaped instance. A dispatch share computed from an unforced outcome field is a plausible number rather than evidence. A control would drive each exit on a fixture and assert the counter, the way `check_sol_observe.py` does for the other recorder |
| **an armed Sol route record joins `/history` by `prompt_id`, labels every DiT block of every forward, and mislabels no non-DiT call** | **`bench/grade_sol_record.py`, by hand, on a record**: it needs an armed render, so nothing runs it unprompted. Every assertion held on its first run (2026-09-01, the canonical PDD graph). The expected refiner and probe row counts are parameters of the grader because both proved graph- and run-dependent |
| **base-en Part One matches the actual I2VA/FL2VA/L2VA mode, final shot, and snapped duration** | **nothing since `0a764a33`**, which removed `bench/red/` and with it the red controls for `preflight_graph.py`'s Part One parse (noted 2026-09-20). `check_speaker_id_control.py` is the live shape for one. Presence-only checking had accepted the I2VA sentence on an FL2VA prompt |
| **the five undocumented markers nest one way only** ([`prompting.md`](../prompting/prompting.md) §7) | **`bench/preflight_graph.py::marker_rules`**, a report rather than a gate, because the prompts this rule most needs to reach are hand-built and never enter `graph_paths()`. Its structural half is asserted; its judgement half (is this caption the right string) is not, and cannot be |
| a speaker is identified where they first appear (base_en 4.4) | **nothing, and not mechanizable.** The camera-motion half of the same guide rule is `check_camera_vocabulary.py`. Two readings of 4.6 (one paragraph; action sounds in the soundscape) are NOT rules; the rules are restated above `LONG_T2V_PROMPT` in `workflows/build_workflows.py`, each marked STATED RULE or NOT A RULE |
| anything patching another pack does it at `execute()` time and counts | **nothing.** Written 2026-08-16 by the session that found the hazard |
| **a PDD arm is consumed by `euler`, not a re-noising sampler** ([`pdd.md`](../distills/pdd.md), "Euler, not `er_sde`") | **nothing.** `check_distill_grid.py` grades the scheduler and `check_distill_settings.py` the shift and step count; no check reads `KSamplerSelect.sampler_name` off a graph. **No escaped instance:** every shipped graph carries `euler`, which is the reason not to build the check yet rather than a reason to call it covered |
| the ref2va checkpoint stays in `MODELS` | **nothing** found |
| **Sol-Attn is on by default in every shipped video workflow** (root `AGENTS.md`) | **`check_attention_defaults.py`** for every graph `graph_paths()` reaches. **Graphs outside `graph_paths()` are not covered**: the escaped instance that prompted the check was a hand-built graph under `internal/`; `bench/preflight_graph.py` reports on those, and reports rather than refuses |
| conditioning rows stay in the block population (`open_experiments` #18) | **`analyze_routing.py`'s `run_controls`**, which prints that `kcvar` spans every block centroid, conditioning included. It needs a capture and exits 2 without one, so it is a documented run rather than a guard anyone can execute |
| the connected-block figures in `sol_curves.py`, [`morton.md`](../attention/morton.md) and the node tooltip | **`analyze_canvas_geometry.py`'s `connected_frac`**, a different implementation that reproduces `morton.md`'s published figures, so agreement is confirmation rather than a tautology. A run, not a check |
| the shared substrate readers have exactly one definition | **nothing, and it is half true.** `git_head` has one implementation: `provenance.py` imports it from `substrate.py` behind an adapter that preserves the stamp's `"not detected"` sentinel, with a try/except ImportError pair because `check_provenance_stamp.py` loads it by path under a bare module name. **Still duplicated:** `_comfy_kitchen_version` and `_sage_version` do the same reads as `substrate._package` in a different shape. Blocked on the schema: those strings are part of the stamp's format, so reshaping them must bump `STAMP_SCHEMA_VERSION` deliberately |
| timings are compared only at equal host GPU power state ([`hardware.md`](../working/hardware.md)) | **captures only.** `provenance.gpu_power_limit_watts` is required in [`capture_manifest_schema.md`](../working/capture_manifest_schema.md) and asserted by `check_capture_manifest.py`. Renders: the stamp has no power, driver or GPU field. Bench runs: `bench/run_graph_arms.py` persists the limit per row since 2026-08-18; `bench/bench_e2e_h3.py` persists nothing. `bench/hwinfo.py` prints the limit and flags a non-stock one, but it is a report a person chooses to run |

## Gaps

One line each. The narrative behind items marked *(pm)* is in
[`check_postmortems.md`](check_postmortems.md).

1. **The `claims block` column is the record.** Where it reads `no`, "what
   breaks if this case is deleted" needs reading the assertions and inferring
   backwards.
2. **No case registry.** `bench/run_checks.py` gives one report per check, but
   most scripts print their own `ok` / `FAIL`, so there is no reliable case count.
3. **`smoke_h3.py` cannot run unattended** -- it needs a live ComfyUI with
   models loaded, and a silently skipped check reads the same as a passing one.
   `check_distill_grid.py` answers this properly, by exiting 2 rather than 0
   when a control did not run. Worth copying.
4. **Controls whose input could not fail** -- four instances, one defect.
   `sol_curves.verify_adjacency` was the clearest, fixed 2026-08-16. *(pm)*
5. **The uncontrolled-requirement audit is one pass deep.** The table above
   covers the old `CLAUDE.md` only; root `AGENTS.md` and `docs/` are unswept. *(pm)*
